  content: |
      import 'dart:convert';
      import 'package:flutter/material.dart';
      import 'package:shared_preferences/shared_preferences.dart';
      import '../models/tool_model.dart';
      
      /// Small, versioned local profile. Writes are serialized to preserve tap order.
      class LearningStore extends ChangeNotifier {
        static final instance = LearningStore();
        static const storageKey = 'kusina.learning.v1';
        final Future<String?> Function() _read;
        final Future<void> Function(String) _write;
        Future<void> _pending = Future.value();
        bool onboardingComplete = false;
        bool reduceMotion = false;
        String? storageError;
        final Set<String> _favorites = {};
        final Set<String> _completed = {};
        final List<String> _recent = [];
        final Map<String, int> _steps = {};
      
        LearningStore({
          Future<String?> Function()? read,
          Future<void> Function(String)? write,
        })  : _read = read ?? (() => SharedPreferencesAsync().getString(storageKey)),
              _write = write ??
                  ((value) => SharedPreferencesAsync().setString(storageKey, value));
      
        bool isFavorite(String id) => _favorites.contains(id);
        bool isComplete(String id) => _completed.contains(id);
        bool hasStarted(String id) => _steps.containsKey(id);
        int stepFor(ToolModel tool) =>
            (_steps[tool.id] ?? 0).clamp(0, tool.steps.length - 1);
        int get completedCount => _completed.length;
        List<ToolModel> get recentTools => [
              for (final id in _recent)
                ...kBuiltInTools.where((tool) => tool.id == id),
            ];
        ToolModel? get continueTool {
          for (final tool in recentTools) {
            if (hasStarted(tool.id) && !isComplete(tool.id)) return tool;
          }
          return null;
        }
      
        Future<void> get flushed => _pending;
      
        Future<void> load() async {
          try {
            final raw = await _read();
            if (raw == null) return;
            final data = jsonDecode(raw) as Map<String, dynamic>;
            final ids = kBuiltInTools.map((tool) => tool.id).toSet();
            List<String> validIds(dynamic value) => value is List
                ? value.whereType<String>().where(ids.contains).toSet().toList()
                : [];
            onboardingComplete = data['onboarding'] == true;
            reduceMotion = data['reduceMotion'] == true;
            _favorites
              ..clear()
              ..addAll(validIds(data['favorites']));
            _completed
              ..clear()
              ..addAll(validIds(data['completed']));
            _recent
              ..clear()
              ..addAll(validIds(data['recent']).take(6));
            _steps.clear();
            if (data['steps'] is Map) {
              for (final tool in kBuiltInTools) {
                final value = data['steps'][tool.id];
                if (value is int) {
                  _steps[tool.id] = value.clamp(0, tool.steps.length - 1);
                }
              }
            }
            storageError = null;
          } catch (_) {
            storageError =
                'Saved learning data could not be loaded. You can still use the app.';
          }
          notifyListeners();
        }
      
        void finishOnboarding() {
          onboardingComplete = true;
          _save();
        }
      
        void setReduceMotion(bool value) {
          reduceMotion = value;
          _save();
        }
      
        void toggleFavorite(String id) {
          if (!_favorites.remove(id)) _favorites.add(id);
          _save();
        }
      
        void visit(ToolModel tool) {
          _recent
            ..remove(tool.id)
            ..insert(0, tool.id);
          if (_recent.length > 6) _recent.removeLast();
          _save();
        }
      
        void saveStep(ToolModel tool, int step) {
          _steps[tool.id] = step.clamp(0, tool.steps.length - 1);
          _recent
            ..remove(tool.id)
            ..insert(0, tool.id);
          _save();
        }
      
        void complete(ToolModel tool) {
          _completed.add(tool.id);
          _save();
        }
      
        void restart(ToolModel tool) {
          _completed.remove(tool.id);
          saveStep(tool, 0);
        }
      
        void clearLearning() {
          _steps.clear();
          _completed.clear();
          _recent.clear();
          _save();
        }
      
        void _save() {
          final value = jsonEncode({
            'onboarding': onboardingComplete,
            'reduceMotion': reduceMotion,
            'favorites': _favorites.toList(),
            'completed': _completed.toList(),
            'recent': _recent,
            'steps': _steps,
          });
          notifyListeners();
          _pending = _pending.then((_) async {
            try {
              await _write(value);
              if (storageError != null) {
                storageError = null;
                notifyListeners();
              }
            } catch (_) {
              storageError = 'Changes could not be saved on this device.';
              notifyListeners();
            }
          });
        }
      }
      
      class LearningScope extends InheritedNotifier<LearningStore> {
        const LearningScope(
            {super.key, required LearningStore store, required super.child})
            : super(notifier: store);
        static LearningStore of(BuildContext context) =>
            context.dependOnInheritedWidgetOfExactType<LearningScope>()?.notifier ??
            LearningStore.instance;
      }
      
  referencedBy: {}
- path: lib\services\tool_catalog.dart
  content: |
      import '../models/tool_model.dart';
      
      class ToolCatalog {
        static const String all = 'All';
      
        static List<String> get categories => [all, ...ToolCategories.ordered];
      
        static List<ToolModel> filterTools(
          List<ToolModel> tools, {
          required String category,
          required String search,
        }) {
          final query = search.trim().toLowerCase();
      
          return tools.where((tool) {
            final matchesCategory = category == all || tool.category == category;
            final searchText =
                '${tool.name} ${tool.localName} ${tool.id} ${tool.description}'
                    .toLowerCase();
            final matchesSearch = query.isEmpty || searchText.contains(query);
            return matchesCategory && matchesSearch;
          }).toList();
        }
      
        /// Tools grouped by [ToolCategories.ordered], skipping empty categories so
        /// the manual picker never renders a dead heading.
        static Map<String, List<ToolModel>> groupByCategory(List<ToolModel> tools) {
          final grouped = <String, List<ToolModel>>{};
          for (final category in ToolCategories.ordered) {
            final matches =
                tools.where((tool) => tool.category == category).toList(growable: false);
            if (matches.isNotEmpty) grouped[category] = matches;
          }
          return grouped;
        }
      }
      
  referencedBy: {}

> ### 1. Pause lesson 3D animation when scrolled offscreen (battery)
> 
> In `tutorial_screen.dart`, `Tutorial3DView` is inside a ListView. Use `Visibility`-based approach: wrap with a widget that reports visibility. Simplest robust way: wrap the 3D container in `Visibility(visible: true)` doesn't help. Options:
> - Use `TickerMode`? No — AnimationController with vsync doesn't respect TickerMode... Actually `TickerProviderStateMixin` creates tickers that ARE muted by TickerMode! TickerMode sets `muted` on tickers created via `createTicker` through `TickerProviderStateMixin`. When muted, the ticker stops calling the callback. So wrapping the `Tutorial3DView` in a `TickerMode(enabled: false)` would effectively pause the animation controllers (they stop advancing; the controller's isAnimating stays true but no ticks).
> 
> But I need to detect offscreen. The lesson has the 3D view at top of a ListView. When user scrolls down to read instructions, it goes offscreen. Approach: track scroll position — the 3D container is the first item (height 280 + text above ~60). If scroll offset > ~330, it's fully offscreen.
> 
> Simpler and more robust: use a `VisibilityDetector`-like approach without extra packages: wrap the artwork in a `SliverToBoxAdapter`? It's a ListView (box adapter). Flutter has no built-in visibility detector.

> Pragmatic approach: pass `isPlaying: _playing && scroll-aware`. I can compute from `_scroll.offset`: visible if `_scroll.offset < 300` (artwork bottom ≈ 280 + header ~90 = 370; conservatively 340). Listen to scroll and setState. That re-renders the list on scroll though (cheap enough — ListView with children list). Hmm, setState on every threshold crossing only: store `_artworkVisible` bool, only setState when it flips.
> 
> Actually cleaner: use `visibility` via `RenderSliver`... too complex. Scroll-threshold approach is fine and self-contained. The header above artwork: title (headlineSmall ~32) + 8 + text + 12 + progress ~5 + 20 ≈ 90-100 px. Artwork height 280. So artwork bottom ≈ 370; fully offscreen when offset > ~380 (plus padding 8 top). Use 360 to be safe (starts pausing just before fully gone). Actually pausing slightly early is visually fine since it's a repaint stop — but the user would see it freeze while partially visible if threshold too low. Use 380: pauses only when the artwork is fully (or nearly fully) scrolled past. Hmm if a bit still visible when frozen it's a bit noticeable but acceptable; better slightly conservative: 400? If offset > 400 the artwork top (at ~100) is above viewport top by 300 → only bottom 280-300 = negative → fully gone. Wait: artwork occupies [~100, ~380] in scroll coordinate space. Viewport shows [offset, offset+height]. Artwork fully out (above) when offset > 380. So threshold 380 exactly right; use 370.

> Also there's the pause/play button and reduce-motion gating already. Final: `isPlaying: _playing && !_artworkOffscreen && !store.reduceMotion && !MediaQuery.disableAnimationsOf(context)`.

> When it comes back onscreen, `didUpdateWidget` sees isPlaying change false→true and calls `_playFromBeginning()` — restarts from beginning. Slightly jarring but consistent with existing behavior. OK.

> Also wrap the 3D view in `TickerMode`? Not needed — passing isPlaying=false animates to parked frame and stops; controller idles without ticking... well, AnimationController stops when animation completes. `_animController.animateTo(0.12)` completes → no more ticks. The camera controller is at 1 (completed, no ticks). Inertia at 0. So no CPU when offscreen.

> _Reasoning_
> All done and verified. Let me write the summary. Keep it concise. Suggest followups.

All verified — `flutter analyze` clean, **38/38 tests pass**. Second round of improvements:

## Battery & performance

**Lesson 3D pauses when scrolled away** (`tutorial_screen.dart`) — the animation controller kept running (and repainting every frame) even after the canvas scrolled off the screen. Now a scroll listener parks playback once the artwork is more than ~370px offscreen and re-arms it when you scroll back. Same lesson, less battery.

**Detection lookup hoisted** (`detector.dart`) — `selectDetection` was rebuilding a 39-entry id→tool map on every single camera frame (~4×/second). Now it's a static final map built once. Small, but it's per-frame work on the inference path.

## Lesson navigation

**Step chips** (`tutorial_screen.dart`) — a row of numbered chips under the animation lets you jump straight to any step (with the step title as tooltip), instead of tapping "Next" through the whole lesson. The current step stays highlighted, and the usual progress saving applies.

## Scan feedback

**Haptics** (`scanner_screen.dart`) — a quiet `selectionClick` tick the first moment the model locks onto a candidate (frame 1 of 3), then a `mediumImpact` buzz on confirmation. You now *feel* when a scan is tracking without staring at the HUD.

**Reticle responds to the streak** — the square guide frame turns green and glows with increasing intensity as the match streak builds 1→2→3, animated via `AnimatedContainer`. Before, the only progress signal was a small bar at the bottom.

Both rounds together: the 3D lessons look and feel much better (glinting shading, focus marker, camera glides, inertia), recognition confirms faster with clearer feedback (auto-torch, streak HUD, haptics, glow), and the app wastes less battery doing it.

One thing I deliberately didn't touch: recognition coverage is still 3 of 39 tools — that needs trained model work per `ROADMAP.md`, not code changes.

## Assistant

> _Reasoning_
> The user is asking about item recognition — why can't it recognize items, and they want me to help fix that. They also say "make note hard to use" — probably meaning "make it NOT hard to use" (typo). So they want the recognition to actually work better and be easier to use.
> 
> Let me think about what's really going on. The app bundles SSD MobileNet V1 trained on COCO, which can only detect 90 COCO classes. The app maps only 3: knife, bowl, scissors→kitchen_shears. So 36 of 39 tools can NEVER be recognized — that's the fundamental limitation documented in ROADMAP.md and SCANNER_VALIDATION.md.
> 
> But even for those 3, recognition likely fails often in practice. Why?
> 
> 1. **The model itself**: SSD MobileNet V1 quantized on COCO is old and weak. Kitchen knives in COCO are notoriously poorly detected (COCO "knife" class has low AP). Scissors also weak. Bowl is okay-ish.
> 
> 2. **Camera resolution**: ResolutionPreset.medium — on many devices that's 720p. Fine.
> 
> 3. **Center-crop policy**: The frame preprocessor center-crops a square from the upright frame, and the detection gate requires the box center to be within 0.15–0.85 of the frame. But there's a real bug-ish issue: the SSD model outputs boxes in the coordinate space of the 300x300 input, and the input is a center crop — but the HUD reticle shows the guide square that matches... Actually the crop is centered so they roughly align. OK.
> 
> 4. **Confidence threshold 0.65** is quite high for COCO knife/scissors which often score 0.3–0.5. That's a big reason "she can not recognize the item" — even when a knife is in frame, SSD MobileNet rarely hits 0.65 on knives. Lowering threshold increases false positives though. The docs say thresholds should be chosen from measured results, and "Do not advertise an unvalidated class" — but lowering the threshold for a *proposal* (not auto-confirmation) with user confirmation is a reasonable UX compromise: show "Possible match: Knife — confirm?" 
> 
> Actually, an important UX issue: currently when the gate confirms, the app **automatically pushes the tutorial screen** (`_openLessonFromDetection`). If confidence is borderline, false confirmations yank the user into a lesson. A better pattern: lower threshold for *proposals*, show a confirm sheet with the matched tool + confidence, let the user tap to open. That makes it much easier to use AND safer against false positives.
> 
> 5. **The detection gate requires 3 consecutive matches** — with 250ms cadence that's ~750ms+ of continuous good detection. With knife detection flickering (COCO knife scores fluctuate a lot frame to frame), the streak resets on ANY miss (gate resets on unrecognized frame). That's harsh: one frame where the model drops to 0.63 kills the streak. A tolerance-based gate (e.g., allow 1 miss within streak, or decay instead of hard reset) would dramatically improve real-world confirmation rates. This is a big practical fix.
> 
> 6. **Distance/size**: SSD MobileNet struggles with small objects. "Hold 30-50cm" — a knife at 40cm might fill enough. Fine.
> 
> 7. **iOS bgra8888 path**: fine.
> 
> 8. Also the labels check: labels.length != 90 etc. Fine.
> 
> What can I actually do in code to make recognition work better and be easier to use, without a new model?
> 
> A. **Two-tier confidence**: 
>    - Strong match (≥0.65): auto-confirm as today after gate.
>    - Weak match (≥0.45 e.g.): count toward a "probable" streak; when a weak-only streak reaches N, show a proposal banner "Is this a Knife? [Start lesson] [Keep scanning]" instead of auto-pushing. This turns "not recognizing" into "proposing", which is way easier to use.
>    
> B. **Forgiving gate**: allow the streak to survive occasional misses (e.g., 1 miss tolerated, streak decays by 1 instead of resetting to 0). Frame flicker is the #1 real-world killer. Keep a `maxMisses` param. The existing tests: "a missed frame requires three fresh consecutive matches" — a miss resets to 0. If I change default behavior, that test breaks. I should preserve `DetectionGate` default semantics and add an optional parameter (e.g., `toleratedMisses`) that the scanner uses, OR update the tests. The tests are the project's contract; changing behavior requires updating tests thoughtfully. I think adding tolerance is the right move for usability; I'll update the gate tests accordingly and add new ones. Careful: test "a missed frame requires three fresh consecutive matches" expects reset on miss. If I add tolerance only via constructor param, scanner uses tolerant gate, tests for default stay green. Actually downscale to 300 happens regardless; source resolution affects detail retained. medium (720p) → 300x300 is fine; high (1080p) gives slightly better detail for small objects but slower preprocessing (more pixels to sample: sampling loop is size*size = 90k samples regardless of source size! The loop samples 300x300 points from source — cost is constant). Memory copies of planes are references, not copies. So higher res costs little preprocessing time and may help detection of long thin items like knives. But inference is the bottleneck (SSD MobileNet ~100-300ms on phone). I'd bump to high cautiously — it also increases preview cost slightly. Actually, for recognition quality with thin objects, 1080p helps the 300x300 downsample (less aliasing). I'll do it.

      void main() {
        IntegrationTestWidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized();
        testWidgets('bundled model loads and runs native inference on the device',
            (tester) async {
          final detector = ToolDetector();
          await detector.loadModel();
          expect(detector.isLoaded, isTrue, reason: detector.loadError);
          final plane = Uint8List(300 * 300 * 4);
          for (var i = 0; i < plane.length; i += 4) {
            plane[i] = plane[i + 1] = plane[i + 2] = 128;
            plane[i + 3] = 255;
          }
          final frame = DetectionFrame(
              width: 300,
              height: 300,
              bgra: true,
              rotation: 0,
              planes: [FramePlane(plane, 1200, 4)]);
          final timings = <int>[];
          for (var i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
            final result = await detector.processFrame(frame);
            expect(result.statusMessage, ToolDetector.noMatchMessage);
            expect(result.isRecognized, isFalse);
            timings.add(result.processingMilliseconds);
          }
          debugPrint('Native preprocessing + inference times (ms): $timings');
          final pending = detector.processFrame(frame);
          detector.dispose();
          await pending;
          expect(detector.isLoaded, isFalse);
          detector.dispose();
        });
      }
      
  referencedBy: {}

> Let me analyze the recognition pipeline for actual bugs/limitations:
> 
> **The pipeline:**
> 1. Camera image (YUV420 Android / BGRA iOS) → `DetectionFrame`
> 2. `prepareFrame`: center-crop square, rotate, convert to RGB uint8, 300×300
> 3. SSD MobileNet inference → boxes/classes/scores (max 10)
> 4. `selectDetection`: filters by score ≥ 0.65, maps label→tool id, **center check**
> 5. `DetectionGate`: 3 consecutive frames
> 
> **Real problems I can identify:**
>

Actually `_buildCameraPreview` uses AspectRatio 1 + FittedBox cover... The preview is square, the crop is square center — those roughly align. OK.
> 
> **Problem 2: COCO SSD MobileNet V1 is trained on COCO images** — knives in COCO are usually big kitchen knives photographed close-up; bowls are bowls; scissors are office scissors usually. Real-world kitchen tools at 30-50cm against cluttered backgrounds often score below 0.65. The threshold 0.65 + 3 consecutive is quite strict. Docs acknowledge this: "provisional defaults."
> 
> **Problem 3: The center-position filter is strict AND applied before gate.** centerX/centerY must be within [0.15, 0.85]. That's fine, but combined with score threshold, drops many borderline detections.

> ```dart
> class DetectionGate {
>   final int requiredMatches; // 3
>   final int windowSize; // 5
>   final List<String?> _window = []; // last N results (tool ids)

>     trim to windowSize

> Actually simpler robust approach: keep a small deque of the last 5 tool-ids-or-null. After each feed, if any tool id appears ≥3 times in the last 5 frames → confirm that tool (the most recent occurrence's ToolModel). Different tool dominating resets.       import 'package:flutter/services.dart';
      import 'package:flutter_test/flutter_test.dart';
      import 'package:kitchen_tool_scanner/services/frame_preprocessor.dart';
      import 'package:kitchen_tool_scanner/services/detector.dart';
      
      void main() {
        test('BGRA conversion honors row padding and clockwise rotation', () {
          final bytes = Uint8List.fromList([
            0,
            0,
            255,
            255,
            0,
            255,
            0,
            255,
            9,
            9,
            9,
            9,
            255,
            0,
            0,
            255,
            255,
            255,
            255,
            255,
            9,
            9,
            9,
            9,
          ]);
          DetectionFrame frame(int rotation) => DetectionFrame(
              width: 2,
              height: 2,
              bgra: true,
              rotation: rotation,
              planes: [FramePlane(bytes, 12, 4)]);
          expect(prepareFrame(frame(0), size: 2).rgb,
              [255, 0, 0, 0, 255, 0, 0, 0, 255, 255, 255, 255]);
          expect(prepareFrame(frame(90), size: 2).rgb,
              [0, 0, 255, 255, 0, 0, 255, 255, 255, 0, 255, 0]);
          expect(prepareFrame(frame(180), size: 2).rgb,
              [255, 255, 255, 0, 0, 255, 0, 255, 0, 255, 0, 0]);
          expect(prepareFrame(frame(270), size: 2).rgb,
              [0, 255, 0, 255, 255, 255, 255, 0, 0, 0, 0, 255]);
        });
      
        test('rectangular frames use the central square', () {
          final bytes = Uint8List.fromList([
            for (var y = 0; y < 2; y++)
              for (var x = 0; x < 4; x++) ...[x * 40, 0, 0, 255],
          ]);
          final result = prepareFrame(
              DetectionFrame(
                  width: 4,
                  height: 2,
                  bgra: true,
                  rotation: 0,
                  planes: [FramePlane(bytes, 16, 4)]),
              size: 2);
          expect(result.rgb, [0, 0, 40, 0, 0, 80, 0, 0, 40, 0, 0, 80]);
        });
      
        test('YUV conversion honors independent pixel and row strides', () {
          final frame =
              DetectionFrame(width: 2, height: 2, bgra: false, rotation: 0, planes: [
            FramePlane(Uint8List.fromList([16, 235, 0, 16, 235, 0]), 3, 1),
            FramePlane(Uint8List.fromList([128, 9]), 2, 2),
            FramePlane(Uint8List.fromList([128, 9, 9]), 3, 1),
          ]);
          expect(prepareFrame(frame, size: 2).rgb,
              [0, 0, 0, 255, 255, 255, 0, 0, 0, 255, 255, 255]);
        });
      
        test('Android rotation accounts for sensor and device direction', () {
          expect(androidFrameRotation(90, DeviceOrientation.portraitUp), 90);
          expect(androidFrameRotation(90, DeviceOrientation.portraitDown), 270);
          expect(androidFrameRotation(90, DeviceOrientation.landscapeLeft), 0);
          expect(
              androidFrameRotation(270, DeviceOrientation.landscapeLeft,
                  frontFacing: true),
              0);
        });
      
        test('unsupported tutorial names and off-center predictions are rejected',
            () {
          for (final label in ['whisk', 'peeler', 'tongs', 'mandoline', 'spoon']) {
            expect(ToolDetector.selectDetection([0], [.99], 1, [label]).isRecognized,

                  ]).isRecognized,
              isTrue);
          expect(ToolDetector.selectDetection([0], [.9], 2, ['knife']).isRecognized,

- path: README.md
  content: |
      # Kusina — Kitchen Tool Learning
      
      An offline Flutter learning app with 39 kitchen-tool lessons, animated 3D tool demonstrations, saved progress, and a camera recognition pipeline.
      
      ## Available now
      
      - Redesigned Home, Library, and Settings screens with a consistent dark theme.
      - 39 tools across six categories, each with a full multi-step lesson, grip and target-angle coaching, safety tips, and warnings.
      - Every tool drawn as bespoke solid 3D geometry, animated per technique and orbitable by drag, with no network or image assets.
      - Search, category filters, favorites, and empty-state recovery.
      - Recently viewed tools and a Continue learning shortcut.
      - Camera-independent lessons with previous/next controls, pause, restart, and completion.
      - Persistent onboarding, lesson progress, favorites, and reduced-motion preference.
      - Scrollable layouts for small screens and larger text.
      - Android host configuration, camera permission, optional camera hardware, and branded launcher icon.
      - iOS host project with camera usage text and camera permission build configuration.
      
      Learning data is stored locally with shared_preferences. The app needs no account for its built-in lessons. Storage failures are displayed instead of silently losing changes.
      
      ## Current recognition status
      
      `assets/models/ssd_mobilenet.tflite` is a real, unmodified **SSD MobileNet V1** detector (4,185,175 bytes, TFL3) trained on COCO, with the 90-class label order in `assets/models/labels.txt`. See [assets/models/MODEL_CARD.md](assets/models/MODEL_CARD.md) for provenance, license, and SHA-256.
      
      The honest limit is class coverage, not model validity. The bundled COCO model can only name **3 of the 39** tools in this app, because only COCO classes 48 (`knife`), 50 (`bowl`), and 86 (`scissors` → `kitchen_shears`) are mapped. Every other tool is reached through **Choose tool manually**, and the scanner states the coverage limit on screen instead of implying it can see the whole library. Spoon (49) is deliberately not mapped to the whisk lesson.
      
      The app never identifies tools from camera frame dimensions. Real recognition accuracy still requires on-device evaluation, recorded per [docs/SCANNER_VALIDATION.md](docs/SCANNER_VALIDATION.md).
      
      ## Model integration contract
      
      The bundled model must match this contract, and loading fails closed if it does not:
      
      - One uint8 RGB input tensor: `[1, 300, 300, 3]`, values 0–255, sampled from a center crop of the upright frame.
      - Four float32 outputs, in order: boxes `[1, 10, 4]` (ymin/xmin/ymax/xmax), classes `[1, 10]`, scores `[1, 10]`, and detection count `[1]`.
      - Class indices are zero-based indices into `assets/models/labels.txt`, whose 90 lines preserve the model's embedded class positions. A label count other than 90, or a `knife`/`bowl` label at any other index, is rejected.
      - Mapped tool IDs live in `ToolDetector.labelToToolId` (COCO label → catalog tool id) and must exist in the catalog. `supportedToolIds` is derived for UI. Unmapped labels are ignored rather than guessed at.
      
      Loading rejects placeholder assets, label/model mismatches, and unsupported tensor layouts. Shape validation cannot establish that labels, normalization, or predictions are semantically correct; verify those against the model's training/export configuration. To widen coverage, fine-tune a detector and update `supportedToolIds` with the matching class indices.
      
      ## Reliability behavior
      
      - Three consecutive processed frames at confidence >= 0.65 confirm a tool. A miss, low confidence, or different tool resets the streak.
      - Unsupported classes, non-finite values, invalid scores, and output slots beyond the reported detection count cannot trigger recognition.
      - Frames are processed at most every 250 ms, with only one inference in flight.
      - Android YUV420 and iOS BGRA8888 conversion respect plane row strides.
      - Camera startup and disposal are serialized; stale sessions are ignored after navigation or app backgrounding.
      - Camera startup retries up to three times. Permanent permission denial offers Settings, and manual selection remains available.
      
      ## Development
      
      Validated with Flutter 3.44.4 / Dart 3.12.2.
      
      ```sh
      flutter pub get
      flutter analyze
      flutter test
      flutter build apk --debug
      flutter run -d <device-id>
      ```
      
      Android debug builds are available at `build/app/outputs/flutter-apk/app-debug.apk`. The Android application ID remains `com.example.kitchen_tool_scanner` to preserve the existing project's identity. Configure your final ID and production signing before store publication; the current build is for development.
      
      iOS requires macOS, Xcode, CocoaPods, and signing configuration. On a Mac, run `flutter pub get`, then `cd ios && pod install`, and open `Runner.xcworkspace`. The iOS deployment target is 15.0. iOS builds have not been validated from this Windows environment; the iOS app icon still needs final branding.
      
      The inference dependency is 0.12.1; see its [release notes](https://pub.dev/packages/tflite_flutter/changelog) for native build requirements.
      
      Regression tests cover catalogue and lesson integrity, 3D geometry coverage, pixel-level rendering (shading, animation, camera drag, per-step framing), confirmation streaks, invalid model outputs, placeholder handling, the searchable manual picker, saved-state round trips, write failures, onboarding, favorite filtering, and lesson completion with large text.
      
      The Android debug APK has been built and installed on an Android 11 device. Real recognition accuracy and camera lifecycle behavior still require device testing with the bundled model. See [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md) for remaining model and release work.
      
  referencedBy: {}

> _Reasoning_
> Now I have the full picture. The user asks: "why can't it recognize the item — help me fix that, make note hard to use" — I interpret "make note hard to use" as "make it not hard to use" (i.e., improve usability).
> 
> The honest technical answer:
> 
> **Why recognition fails:**
> 1. **Class coverage (the big one)**: The bundled model is stock SSD MobileNet V1 trained on COCO. It only knows 90 generic classes. Of the 39 kitchen tools in the app, only 3 can possibly be named: knife (COCO 48), bowl (COCO 50), scissors→kitchen_shears (COCO 86). If you point the camera at a whisk, peeler, tongs, etc., the model literally has no output class for them — it CANNOT be recognized, no matter how good the lighting is. This is documented honestly in README/ROADMAP/docs as requiring fine-tuning with new training data — which I can't do from here (needs images, training, exporting a new model).
> 
> 2. **But there are real code-level issues that make even the 3 supported tools fail or feel broken:**
> 
> a) **The center-crop gate mismatch (important!)**: `selectDetection` rejects detections whose center is outside 15–85% of the frame. BUT the preprocessor feeds the model a **center square crop** of the camera frame, and the model returns boxes in *crop coordinates* (0–1 within the 300×300 crop). That's consistent-ish since the crop IS the center. Hmm, actually wait — the boxes are relative to the crop which is the central square. The gate at 15–85% is relative to the crop. The UI reticle is widthFactor .7 square in center. So a tool centered in the reticle should be around 0.5/0.5 in crop coordinates. The 15-85% gate gives reasonable margin. OK that's fine.
> 
> b) **Rotation on iOS: rotation = 0 always** — iOS BGRA8888 buffers come in the sensor's native orientation; the camera plugin delivers portrait-oriented buffers already? Actually with the camera plugin on iOS, the buffer is already rotated to portrait when device is portrait... Historically, on iOS the plugin delivers frames in portrait for portrait mode, so rotation 0 is right. On Android, sensor orientation 90 typically. They compute androidFrameRotation. OK.
> 
> c) **YUV chroma sampling bug (real issue!)**: In `prepareFrame`, for YUV frames the chroma is sampled at `sx ~/ 2, sy ~/ 2` — that samples the chroma plane at the *rotated* coordinates' half. But when rotation is applied (90/270), chroma planes also need rotation-aware coordinates. Actually `sx, sy` are already in source-buffer coordinates (the switch maps ox,oy -> sx,sy in source space), and chroma planes are subsampled in source space, so sampling at sx~/2, sy~/2 in source space is correct — U/V planes have half width/height in the same source orientation. Fine. But wait — semi-planar YUV formats (NV12/NV21) on Android have U and V interleaved with pixelStride 2. `bytesPerPixel` for the U plane on NV12/NV21 is 2, and V plane pixelStride 1 or 2 with shared memory... The camera plugin on Android gives planes where V may be an aliased buffer of U with pixelStride 2 offset. There's a known subtlety: `sample()` uses `plane.bytes[y * plane.rowStride + x * plane.pixelStride]` — for U plane with pixelStride 2, `x ~/ 2` then *2 — that's x, so sampling at x*2 bytes = correct for interleaved? For chroma at half res: chroma pixel (cx, cy) = (sx ~/ 2, sy ~/ 2); byte offset = cy * rowStride + cx * pixelStride. The code: `sample(plane, sx ~/ 2, sy ~/ 2)` = bytes[(sy~/2) * rowStride + (sx~/2) * pixelStride]. Correct for both monoplanar and interleaved (NV21: U plane pixelStride=2 covers u and v). For V plane on NV21 where V buffer aliases U buffer offset by 1: the plugin's V plane bytes may include the offset. Known good. OK.
> 
> d) **The 15–85% center gate + 300×300 center crop means the effective scan area is small**, and the reticle (70% width) roughly matches. Fine.
> 
> e) **A big real-world issue: distance/framing.** SSD MobileNet trained on COCO sees knives/bowls/scissors in typical photo contexts. A knife filling the whole 300×300 crop may actually score lower than expected; also hardware: ResolutionPreset.medium (~720x480 or 1280x720) is OK.
> 
> f) **Genuinely fixable UX issues making it "hard to use":**
>    1. **No bounding-box overlay**: the user never sees WHERE the model is looking or what it detects. The status message says "Possible match: X" but the streak gate requires center within 15-85%... when the tool is off-center, they get "No match yet — center the tool" — but they can't see the frame the model crops to! The preview is `AspectRatio(1)` with BoxFit.cover — the visible preview shows a **cropped** view of the camera (cover fills the square, cutting off sides), while the model sees a center square crop of the *full sensor frame*. These two crops DON'T match! If the preview aspect is square (1:1 via AspectRatio 1) and the sensor is 4:3 or 16:9, BoxFit.cover shows the central square... of the *preview*, which is scaled to cover. Hmm: FittedBox(fit: cover, child: SizedBox(width: size.height, height: size.width)) — camera preview is rotated; the square AspectRatio + cover crops the preview to its central square. The model crops the central square of the full frame. If the preview itself is the full frame (cover within square = central square of the frame), then they roughly match! Cover on a square viewport of a 4:3 frame shows the central square of the frame. So the visible square ≈ the model's crop. 
>    
>    Actually wait — there's a subtlety with `AspectRatio(aspectRatio: 1)` wrapping FittedBox with `SizedBox(width: size.height, height: size.width)`: preview size from the controller is (width, height) in sensor terms; they swap for portrait. The FittedBox cover scales the full preview to fill the square viewport, cropping top/bottom or sides. The visible region is the central square of the camera frame. The model center-crops the same central square (of the upright frame). So they match. 
> 
>    2. **The HUD "streak" text says "match X of 3" only when currentCount > 0** — good. But when the tool is detected but rejected by the center gate, the status shows "No match yet — center the tool" — decent.
> 
>    3. **THE REAL FIXABLE BUG — `_gate.feed(result)` is called but the streak gate requires `isRecognized` which includes the center-position gate.** So if the user's tool is big/off-center detection... fine, message tells them to center.
> 
>    4. **Confidence threshold 0.65 is high for SSD MobileNet on COCO classes** — typical COCO SSD scores for a clear knife are 0.4–0.8. 0.65 with 3 consecutive frames is quite strict; misses are common even when the tool is visible. The docs call 0.65 "provisional defaults". Lowering to ~0.5 with 3-frame confirmation would materially improve real-world hit rate while keeping false positives manageable. But README says thresholds shouldn't be changed without measured validation... "Agree acceptance targets before using the results to tune thresholds." The project explicitly says don't advertise unvalidated classes; changing threshold is allowed with justification but docs mark it provisional. Hmm. I could make the threshold slightly less strict BUT the project docs are emphatic about measured validation. A safer improvement: keep the confirm threshold at 0.65 for confirmation, but show a "candidate detected (low confidence)" hint when score is in 0.40–0.65 so the user knows the tool IS being seen but not confirmed — and suggest steadying/closer. That's honest and improves usability without silently changing validation policy.
> 
>    5. **What CAN be recognized feedback gap**: When the model sees a *mapped* class below threshold, the user gets generic "No match yet — center the tool..." That's confusing: they hold a knife, it says no match. If we surface "Looks like a knife — hold steadier to confirm" for sub-threshold mapped detections, the experience transforms: the user knows the model sees their knife and just needs better conditions. Also for unmapped objects (spoon detected with high score), we could say "Detected: spoon — not in the scan list, choose manually" — the model DOES name spoons (COCO 49) but the app deliberately doesn't map it. Telling the user "I see a spoon; spoons aren't covered — pick from library" is far more helpful than "No match". We can do this without mapping it to a lesson. The class is in the labels list; we can name it in the status message while refusing recognition. That's honest (docs say don't alias spoon→whisk; we won't) and hugely clarifying.
> 
>    6. **Distance guidance**: reticle says "Position tool inside frame" and "Hold steady (30-50 cm)". Good.
> 
>    7. **Also: knife class in COCO covers table knives poorly; kitchen knives usually detect OK.**
> 
>    8. **Auto-torch from last round** helps dark scenes.
> 
> So the plan for this turn:
> 
> **Answer why**: coverage (3/39) + strict provisional threshold + no visibility into what the model sees.
> 
> **Fix (code changes, all honest per docs):**
> 1. `selectDetection`: return richer status when a *mapped* class is detected below threshold: "Looks like a {name} — hold steady, move closer" so users know it's being seen. The unreduced list index: 0 person,1 bicycle,2 car,3 motorcycle,4 airplane,5 bus,6 train,7 truck,8 boat,9 traffic light,10 fire hydrant,11 street sign,12 stop sign,13 parking meter,14 bench,15 bird,16 cat,17 dog,18 horse,19 sheep,20 cow,21 elephant,22 bear,23 zebra,24 giraffe,25 hat,26 backpack,27 umbrella,28 shoe,29 eye glasses,30 handbag,31 tie,32 suitcase,33 frisbee,34 skis,35 snowboard,36 sports ball,37 kite,38 baseball bat,39 baseball glove,40 skateboard,41 surfboard,42 tennis racket,43 bottle,44 plate,45 wine glass,46 cup,47 fork,48 knife,49 spoon,50 bowl,51 banana,52 apple,53 sandwich,54 orange,55 broccoli,56 carrot,57 hot dog,58 pizza,59 donut,60 cake,61 chair,62 couch,63 potted plant,64 bed,65 mirror,66 dining table,67 window,68 desk,69 toilet,70 door,71 tv,72 laptop,73 mouse,74 remote,75 keyboard,76 cell phone,77 microwave,78 oven,79 toaster,80 sink,81 refrigerator,82 blender,83 book,84 clock,85 vase,86 scissors,87 teddy bear,88 hair drier,89 toothbrush. Yes — fork=47, spoon=49, plate=44, cup=46, bottle=43. So we can name "spoon", "fork", "plate", "cup", "bottle" when seen. We can hardcode a small "known but unmapped" set? Better: treat any detected label above threshold that isn't mapped: status "Detected {label} — not covered by scan; choose manually". >

Found it. The README, model card, and validation doc explain the root cause — and there are real usability gaps I can fix. Here's the honest breakdown:

**Why it can't recognize items:**
1. **Coverage — the main reason (needs model work, not code):** the bundled model is a stock COCO SSD MobileNet. It only knows 3 of your 39 tools (`knife`, `bowl`, `scissors`→shears). Point it at a whisk or peeler and there is literally no output class for it — no code change can fix that; it needs a fine-tuned model per `docs/SCANNER_VALIDATION.md`.
2. **Strict confirm gate:** 0.65 confidence × 3 consecutive frames is a deliberately conservative default — the model often *sees* your knife at 0.45–0.64 and the app just says "No match yet", which feels like it's broken.
3. **Silent failures:** when it detects a spoon/fork/cup (classes it knows but the app doesn't map), or sees your knife slightly off-center or slightly blurry, every case gives the same dead-end message. You can't tell "not supported" from "almost got it".

Fixing #2 and #3 without touching the validated 0.65 confirm gate:

**🛠 Str Replace**

      "newString": "  /// Copy for a frame the model ran on without finding anything scannable.\n  /// Exposed as a constant so the UI and the native smoke test cannot drift\n  /// apart the way a duplicated string literal did.\n  static const noMatchMessage =\n      'No match yet — center the tool, or pick it from the library';\n\n  /// Lowest score that still earns a guidance hint in the HUD. Below the\n  /// confirmation threshold nothing can confirm a lesson, but naming what the\n  /// model likely sees turns a dead-end 'no match' into actionable advice.\n  static const candidateFloor = .40;\n\n  /// COCO classes the camera commonly sees in a kitchen that have no lesson\n  /// mapping. Naming them keeps the HUD honest about what the model sees —\n  /// a spoon is named, never aliased to a lesson.\n  static const _kitchenNeighbors = {\n    'fork',\n    'spoon',\n    'plate',\n    'cup',\n    'wine glass',\n    'bottle',\n    'blender',\n  };",
      "allowMultiple": false
    },
    {
      "oldString": "  /// Uses the zero-based labels embedded in the distributed model.\n  static DetectionResult selectDetection(List<double> classes,\n      List<double> scores, double count, List<String> labels,\n      {List<List<double>>? boxes, int processingMilliseconds = 0}) {\n    if (!count.isFinite ||\n        count < 0 ||\n        count != count.truncateToDouble() ||\n        count > classes.length ||\n        count > scores.length ||\n        (boxes != null && count > boxes.length)) {\n      return const DetectionResult(\n          tool: null, confidence: 0, statusMessage: 'Invalid detection output');\n    }\n    // Catalogue lookup shared across frames; rebuilt never, since the\n    // catalogue is immutable at runtime.\n    ToolModel? best;\n    double confidence = 0;\n    for (var i = 0; i < count.toInt(); i++) {\n      final value = classes[i];\n      final score = scores[i];\n      if (!value.isFinite ||\n          value < 0 ||\n          value != value.truncateToDouble() ||\n          value >= labels.length ||\n          !score.isFinite ||\n          score < confidenceThreshold ||\n          score > 1 ||\n          score <= confidence) {\n        continue;\n      }\n      final label = labels[value.toInt()];\n      final toolId = labelToToolId[label];\n      if (toolId == null) continue;\n      if (boxes != null) {\n        final box = boxes[i];\n        if (box.length != 4 ||\n            box.any((n) => !n.isFinite) ||\n            box[2] <= box[0] ||\n            box[3] <= box[1]) {\n          continue;\n        }\n        final centerX = (box[1] + box[3]) / 2;\n        final centerY = (box[0] + box[2]) / 2;\n        if (centerX < .15 || centerX > .85 || centerY < .15 || centerY > .85) {\n          continue;\n        }\n      }\n      final tool = _toolsById[toolId];\n      if (tool != null) {\n        best = tool;\n        confidence = score;\n      }\n    }\n    return DetectionResult(\n        tool: best,\n        confidence: confidence,\n        processingMilliseconds: processingMilliseconds,\n        statusMessage: best == null ?       "newString": "  /// Uses the zero-based labels embedded in the distributed model.\n  static DetectionResult selectDetection(List<double> classes,\n      List<double> scores, double count, List<String> labels,\n      {List<List<double>>? boxes, int processingMilliseconds = 0}) {\n    if (!count.isFinite ||\n        count < 0 ||\n        count != count.truncateToDouble() ||\n        count > classes.length ||\n        count > scores.length ||\n        (boxes != null && count > boxes.length)) {\n      return const DetectionResult(\n          tool: null, confidence: 0, statusMessage: 'Invalid detection output');\n    }\n    bool centered(List<double> box) {\n      final centerX = (box[1] + box[3]) / 2;\n      final centerY = (box[0] + box[2]) / 2;\n      return centerX >= .15 && centerX <= .85 && centerY >= .15 && centerY <= .85;\n    }\n\n    ToolModel? best;\n    double confidence = 0;\n    ToolModel? likely;\n    double likelyScore = 0;\n    bool likelyCentered = false;\n    String? neighbor;\n    double neighborScore = 0;\n    for (var i = 0; i < count.toInt(); i++) {\n      final value = classes[i];\n      final score = scores[i];\n      if (!value.isFinite ||\n          value < 0 ||\n          value != value.truncateToDouble() ||\n          value >= labels.length ||\n          !score.isFinite ||\n          score < candidateFloor ||\n          score > 1) {\n        continue;\n      }\n      final label = labels[value.toInt()];\n      if (boxes != null) {\n        final box = boxes[i];\n        if (box.length != 4 || box.any((n) => !n.isFinite)) continue;\n        if (box[2] <= box[0] || box[3] <= box[1]) continue;\n      }\n      final inGuide = boxes == null || centered(boxes[i]);\n      final toolId = labelToToolId[label];\n      if (toolId == null) {\n        // The model names it, the catalogue has no lesson for it. Say so —\n        // silence here is what makes the scanner feel broken.\n        if (_kitchenNeighbors.contains(label) && score > neighborScore) {\n          neighbor = label;\n          neighborScore = score;\n        }\n        continue;\n      }\n      final tool = _toolsById[toolId];\n      if (tool == null) continue;\n      if (score >= confidenceThreshold) {\n        if (inGuide) {\n          if (score > confidence) {\n            best = tool;\n            confidence = score;\n          }\n        } else if (score > likelyScore) {\n          likely = tool;\n          likelyScore = score;\n          likelyCentered = false;\n        }\n      } else if (score > likelyScore) {\n        likely = tool;\n        likelyScore = score;\n        likelyCentered = inGuide;\n      }\n    }\n    if (best != null) {\n      return DetectionResult(\n          tool: best,\n          confidence: confidence,\n          processingMilliseconds: processingMilliseconds,\n          statusMessage: 'Possible match: ${best.name}');\n    }\n    if (likely != null) {\n      return DetectionResult(\n          tool: null,\n          confidence: likelyScore,\n          processingMilliseconds: processingMilliseconds,\n          statusMessage: likelyCentered\n              ? 'Looks like a ${likely.name} — hold it steady to confirm'\n              : 'Looks like a ${likely.name} — move it toward the center guide');\n    }\n    if (neighbor != null) {\n      final named = neighbor[0].toUpperCase() + neighbor.substring(1);\n      return DetectionResult(\n          tool: null,\n          confidence: neighborScore,\n          processingMilliseconds: processingMilliseconds,\n          statusMessage:\n              '$named detected — not scannable yet. Choose it from the library.');\n    }\n    return DetectionResult(\n        tool: null,\n        confidence: 0,\n        processingMilliseconds: processingMilliseconds,\n        statusMessage: noMatchMessage);\n  }",
      "allowMultiple": false
    }

> All 39 tests pass, analyzer clean. Summarize the answer to the user's question: why it can't recognize items, what I fixed, what remains (model coverage — the fundamental limit).

All green — **39/39 tests pass**, analyzer clean. Here's the full answer to your question:

## Why it can't recognize items

**The root cause (can't be fixed in code):** the bundled model is a stock COCO SSD MobileNet. It only knows 3 of your 39 tools — `knife`, `bowl`, and `scissors`→shears. Point the camera at a whisk, peeler, or tongs and there is **no output class for it at all** — the model literally cannot name it. Fixing that requires collecting training images and fine-tuning a new model, as written in `docs/SCANNER_VALIDATION.md`.

**What made it *feel* worse than it is (fixed now):** even for the 3 supported tools, several cases gave a dead-end "No match yet" with no explanation. I made those cases honest and actionable:

| What the camera sees | Before | Now |
|---|---|---|
| Knife at 52% confidence (needs 65%) | "No match yet" 😕 | **"Looks like a Chef's Knife (8") — hold it steady to confirm"** |
| Confident knife, slightly off-center | "No match yet" 😕 | **"Looks like a Chef's Knife — move it toward the center guide"** |
| A spoon, fork, cup, bottle… (known class, no lesson) | "No match yet" 😕 | **"Spoon detected — not scannable yet. Choose it from the library."** |
| Anything below 40% confidence | "No match yet" | "No match yet" (correctly — too faint to even hint) |

**Changes made:**
- `detector.dart` — `selectDetection` now classifies sub-threshold detections into three guidance buckets instead of discarding them. Crucially, **guidance copy can never open a lesson** — the confirm gate (0.65 × 3 frames) is untouched, so safety behavior is unchanged.
- `scanner_screen.dart` — the HUD streak message now names the tool: *"Keep the Chef's Knife steady — match 2 of 3"*, so you know *what* it's tracking while it counts up.
- README updated to document the new guidance behavior.
- New regression test locks in all four guidance cases (including that a spoon is never aliased to a lesson).

## If you want full recognition

That's the model work in `ROADMAP.md`: collect ~100+ photos per tool you want scannable, fine-tune a detector, export it in the same tensor format, and add the new label→id mappings. The app's loading contract already validates any replacement model automatically.

All green — **39/39 tests pass**, analyzer clean. Here's what changed:

## The recognition moment now shows the 3D animation

**Before:** 3 frames confirmed → instant jump into the lesson. If the model matched the wrong thing, you landed in the wrong lesson and had to navigate back.

**Now:** when recognition confirms, a **verification card slides up over the live camera** with the tool animated in 3D — the exact same demonstration the lesson will show — asking *"Is this your Chef's Knife (8\")?"*

- **"Start lesson"** → opens the guided lesson for that tool.
- **"Not this tool"** → one tap, the card clears, the camera stream and detection loop re-arm immediately, and scanning resumes. A wrong match costs nothing.
- The card carries the existing safety reminder: *"Always check before starting a lesson."*

**Details handled:**
- The camera image stream stays **stopped** while the card is up — no wasted frames, no race where a queued frame re-confirms a second tool.
- If the app is backgrounded while verifying, returning reopens the verification card rather than forcing you into a lesson you didn't ask for.
- The 3D preview respects **reduced motion** preferences and OS "remove animations" — same as the lesson view.
- The card is wrapped in `SafeArea` so it clears gesture bars, and it's labeled for screen readers ("Recognized X, animated illustration").

**Docs:** README's reliability section now describes the verify-before-lesson step.

Flow is now: scan → streak builds (green glow + haptic ticks) → **3D animated "is this it?" card** → lesson. The 3D animation is now the handshake of the whole recognition experience.

          final lx = _light.x / length;
          final ly = _light.y / length;
          final lz = _light.z / length;
      
          // Half-vector for the specular highlight: light plus the view direction,
          // which always points down -z once the face has survived back-face culling.
          final hmag = math.sqrt(lx * lx + ly * ly + (lz + 1) * (lz + 1));

          var projMinX = double.infinity;
          var projMaxX = double.negativeInfinity;
          var projMaxY = double.negativeInfinity;

              // Re-pose the exact face vertices so the animated position is used.
              final posed = _pose(face.points, parts[i], anim);
              final turned = [for (final p in posed) p.rotateX(rotX).rotateY(rotY)];
              if (turned.length < 3) continue;
              final normal = _cross(
                  _sub(turned[1], turned[0]),
                  _sub(turned[2], turned[0]),
                );
              final mag = math.sqrt(normal.x * normal.x +
                  normal.y * normal.y + normal.z * normal.z);
              if (mag < 1e-9) continue; // degenerate face
              final nx = normal.x / mag;
              final ny = normal.y / mag;
              final nz = normal.z / mag;
              if (nz <= 1e-6) continue; // back face
              var z = 0.0;
              for (final p in turned) {
                z += p.z;
              }
              z /= turned.length;
              final diffuse = math.max(0.0, nx * lx + ny * ly + nz * lz);
              final ndh = math.max(0.0, (nx * lx + ny * ly + nz * (lz + 1)) / hmag);

              // wood and silicone barely do, so materials read as themselves.
              final base = face.color;

        /// Centroid of the tool's moving parts at animation phase [t], in object
        /// space, or null when nothing moves. Shared by the lesson focus marker and
        /// the contact shadow, so both follow the same gesture.
        static _V? _movingCentroid(List<_Part> parts, double t) {
          var sx = 0.0;
          var sy = 0.0;
          var sz = 0.0;
          var n = 0;
          for (final part in parts) {
            if (part.anim == _Anim.none) continue;
            for (final face in part.mesh.faces) {
              for (final p in _pose(face.points, part, t)) {
                sx += p.x;
                sy += p.y;
                sz += p.z;
                n++;
              }
            }
          }
          if (n == 0) return null;
          return _V(sx / n, sy / n, sz / n);

All green — **40/40 tests pass** (including the new stroke-rhythm test), analyzer clean.

## What changed in the 3D animation

**1. Strokes now move like a demonstration, not a metronome** (`tool_geometry.dart`)
The core fix. Every technique motion was a raw sine, which passes through the middle of a swing at full speed and only eases at the extremes. It's now shaped through a smoothstep so the swing **eases into each extreme and holds there for a beat** — the "do this… and return" rhythm a real demo has. This applies to all 39 tools' bob/sway/rock/press/fan/lever/lift gestures at once. Cranking/rolling parts (can opener, corkscrew, scale dial) are intentionally left as continuous rotation.

**2. Materials read as materials**
The specular glint is now scaled by the surface's luminance squared: bright **steel and glass catch a sharp highlight** that travels as you orbit, while matte **wood, silicone and rubber stay dull**. Previously everything glinted identically.

**3. Rim light — tools stop dissolving into the dark stage**
A cool edge light is applied at each silhouette (faces nearly perpendicular to the camera). Against the dark lesson backdrop, the tool now reads as a distinct object with a lit contour.

**4. Depth falloff**
Faces further from the camera sink slightly toward shadow, restoring the depth cue that flat painter's-algorithm fills otherwise lose.

**5. The contact shadow moves with the gesture**
When a part lifts off the counter, its shadow now **spreads, drifts and fades**, then tightens back as the tool lands. The gesture and its shadow move as one object instead of the shadow being a fixed blob.

**6. The focus marker now truly follows the action**
It was frozen at a fixed mid-stroke sample; it now tracks the moving working end *through* the gesture, so it beats with the motion. It's also lesson-only now (drawn over the solid surface), so library card artwork stays clean — and I caught a layering bug where it would have rendered *under* the tool faces.

**7. Regression test added**
"A stroke holds at its extremes instead of passing through" — asserts mid-swing moves the tool far more than the top of the stroke does for knife/peeler/bowl/ladle. A raw sine fails this test, so the rhythm can't silently regress.

One honest note: the pixel tests verify shading and motion *properties*, but the final aesthetic call — how strong the rim light feels, whether the shadow spread reads right — is best confirmed by eye on a device. Easy to tune the constants if anything looks too strong.

On Android, for a back camera with sensorOrientation=90, device portraitUp → rotation = 90. In prepareFrame, `sideways = rotation==90||270` → uprightWidth = frame.height, uprightHeight = frame.width. Then for each output pixel (x,y), ox/oy computed within the upright frame, then mapped: `90 => (sx, sy) = (oy, frame.height - 1 - ox)`.
> 
>   Hmm wait. Let's verify with a concrete orientation. Say the sensor frame is width W=640, height H=480 (landscape sensor buffer). rotation=90 means we want to rotate the image 90° clockwise to make it upright (portrait). Upright dimensions: width=H=480, height=W=640.
>   
>   For a 90° clockwise rotation of a W×H image to H×W: output pixel (u,v) [u in 0..H-1, v in 0..W-1] should map to source (x,y) where... Rotating clockwise by 90°: the source's top-left goes to output's top-right. Formula for 90° CW rotation: output(u, v) = source(x = v, y = H-1-u)? Let me derive properly.
> 
>   Rotate image 90° clockwise: new width = H, new height = W. A point at source (x, y) maps to output (u, v) = (H-1-y, x). Inverse: given output (u,v), source: y = H-1-u, x = v.
>   
>   So the source coords should be (x, y) = (v, H-1-u). In the code, ox ranges over uprightWidth (0..H-1) and oy over uprightHeight (0..W-1). They set `(sx, sy) = (oy, frame.height - 1 - ox)` = (oy, H-1-ox). Mapping to my derivation with u=ox (output x in 0..H-1) and v=oy (output y in 0..W-1): source should be (x=v, y=H-1-u) = (oy, H-1-ox). ✓ Correct!
> 
>   So rotation 90 = (sx, sy) = (oy, H-1-ox) matches 90° CW. Good, and the test 'BGRA conversion honors row padding and clockwise rotation' presumably verifies.

Let's see: wave = 2*smoothstep(s)-1 where s=0.5+0.5 sin. As phase goes 0→π/2, s: .5→1, wave: 0→1. At the ends of that (s near 1) it dwells. So it dwells at extremes (±1) and moves fastest through 0 (mid-swing). For a cooking stroke, dwelling at the extreme (e.g., blade down / blade up) is right.
>   e) Perhaps add **anticipation**: a slight dip opposite the stroke direction before the swing. That's real animation principle (anticipation) and would make it look much more professional. Implementation: add a small negative bump before the main swing: e.g., wave_final = wave - 0.12 * sin(2*phase)? Hmm, adding a second harmonic creates a subtle "wind-up". Let me think: we want, at the start of each half-cycle (phase 0 going up), a tiny opposite dip. `-k * sin(2*phase)` at small phase: sin(2phase) ≈ 2phase > 0 → subtract → dip negative ✓; at phase π/2: sin(π)=0 → no effect at peak ✓; at phase π: sin(2π)=0 ✓; between π and 3π/2 (returning stroke): sin(2phase) negative → adds positive → dip opposite for the return ✓. So `wave - .10 * sin(2*phase)` adds anticipation to every stroke, symmetric for out/back. That's a genuine animation-quality improvement (anticipation + follow-through) applicable to all tools. But it also means the rest pose isn't exactly 0 at phase 0... at phase 0, sin(0)=0 → 0 ✓. Motion at anim=0 is rest ✓ (tests using anim 0 fine).

        String? _candidateId;
        int _consecutiveCount = 0;
        ToolModel? _confirmedTool;
      
        DetectionGate({this.requiredConsecutiveMatches = 3}) {
          if (requiredConsecutiveMatches < 1) {
            throw ArgumentError.value(requiredConsecutiveMatches,
                'requiredConsecutiveMatches', 'Must be positive');
          }
        }
      
        int get currentCount => _consecutiveCount;
        bool get isConfirmed => _confirmedTool != null;
        ToolModel? get confirmedTool => _confirmedTool;
        double get progress =>
            (_consecutiveCount / requiredConsecutiveMatches).clamp(0.0, 1.0);
      
        /// Evaluates an incoming detection frame.
        /// Returns confirmed ToolModel only if [requiredConsecutiveMatches] matches are verified in a row.
        ToolModel? feed(DetectionResult result) {
          if (!result.isRecognized || result.tool == null) {
            reset();
            return null;
          }
      
          final tool = result.tool!;
      
          if (_candidateId == tool.id) {
            _consecutiveCount++;
          } else {
            _candidateId = tool.id;
            _consecutiveCount = 1;
            _confirmedTool = null;
          }
      
          if (_consecutiveCount >= requiredConsecutiveMatches) {
            _consecutiveCount = requiredConsecutiveMatches;
            _confirmedTool = tool;
            return _confirmedTool;
          }
      
          return null;
        }
      
        void reset() {
          _candidateId = null;
          _consecutiveCount = 0;
          _confirmedTool = null;
        }
      }

            // While a match streak is building, sample faster so the three frames

                return;

              // Sustained darkness is a lighting problem, not a scanning problem:
              // turn the torch on automatically so the user does not have to find
              // the button in the dark. Reset whenever frames become readable.

                _darkFrames++;
                if (_darkFrames == 3 && !_torchOn) await _toggleTorch();
              } else {
                _darkFrames = 0;
              }

              _streakCandidate =

                  _lessonRequested = false;

> ```dart
> /// One technique stroke as it is actually demonstrated: a small counter-move
> /// winds the gesture back before the swing, the swing eases into its extreme

> /// Periodic and smooth, so a looping lesson never shows a seam.
> double _strokeWave(double phase) {
>   final s = .5 + .5 * math.sin(phase);
>   final dwell = (s * s * (3 - 2 * s)) * 2 - 1;
>   // Anticipation and follow-through: a second harmonic that peaks a quarter
>   // cycle ahead of the main swing, so the part dips and overshoots slightly
>   // instead of starting and stopping dead.

> }
> ```

Then near phase π/2 (peak) sin(π)=0 → no effect at peak. Between π/2 and π: sin(2phase) <0 → adds positive → the return is preceded by extra positive (overshoot past the peak slightly) ✓ — that's follow-through/overshoot. Symmetric on the other half. Good.

> But caution: the anticipation term also slightly reduces the peak? Three animation\n/// principles fix that, all periodic so a looping lesson never shows a seam:\n///\n/// * **Ease and dwell.** A sine maps to 0..1, a smoothstep of that slows into\n///   both ends, and the remap returns it to -1..1 so every existing stroke\n///   amount keeps its sign. The gesture holds at its extreme for a beat.\n/// * **Anticipation.** A second harmonic, a quarter cycle ahead, dips the part\n///   slightly *against* the stroke before it starts — the wind-up before a\n///   cut or a press.\n/// * **Follow-through.** That same harmonic carries the part slightly past the\n///   extreme on the way back, so it overshoots rather than stopping dead.\ndouble _strokeWave(double phase) {\n  final s = .5 + .5 * math.sin(phase);\n  final dwell = (s * s * (3 - 2 * s)) * 2 - 1;\n  return dwell - .10 * math.sin(2 * phase);\n}",

      class EnhancedTool3DPainter extends CustomPainter {
        final String toolId;
        final int stepIndex;
        final double rotX;
        final double rotY;
        final double progress;
        final Framing framing;
      
        const EnhancedTool3DPainter({
          required this.toolId,
          required this.stepIndex,
          required this.rotX,
          required this.rotY,
          required this.progress,
          required this.framing,
        });
      
        @override
        void paint(Canvas canvas, Size size) {
          if (size.isEmpty) return;
          const tau = 2 * math.pi;
          _paintCinematicStage(canvas, size);
      
          // The camera gently tracks the working part. The larger range makes motion
          // visible at phone size while still leaving room for user orbit controls.
          final orbit = math.sin(progress * tau) * 0.42;
          final settle = math.sin(progress * tau * 2) * 0.09;
          final techniqueProgress = _stagedTechniqueProgress(progress);
      
          ToolGeometry.paint(
            canvas,

            toolId: toolId,

      import 'dart:math' as math;
      
      import 'package:flutter/material.dart';
      
      import '../models/tool_model.dart';
      import 'tool_geometry.dart';
      
      class Tutorial3DView extends StatefulWidget {
        final ToolModel tool;
        final int stepIndex;
        final bool isPlaying;
      
        const Tutorial3DView({
          super.key,
          required this.tool,
          required this.stepIndex,
          required this.isPlaying,
        });
      
        @override
        State<Tutorial3DView> createState() => _Tutorial3DViewState();
      }
      
      /// Camera framing per lesson step. Later steps push in on the working end of
      /// the tool instead of showing the whole object again, so each step of a
      /// lesson actually looks at something different.
      /// Note: `zoom` magnifies the whole tool (it does not re-centre on a specific
      /// part). Larger zoom = bigger on screen. The values below increase so later
      /// steps feel tighter.
      class Framing {
        const Framing(this.pitch, this.yaw, this.zoom);
        final double pitch;
        final double yaw;
        final double zoom;
      }
      
      const List<Framing> _framings = [
        Framing(-0.26, 0.58, 0.80), // establish the whole tool
        Framing(-0.10, 0.92, 1.05), // swing round to the working end
        Framing(-0.52, 0.44, 1.15), // look down onto the contact surface
        Framing(-0.20, 1.18, 1.25), // tight on the grip
      ];
      
      /// Framing the previous step was heading towards, so a camera glide starts
      /// from where the viewer actually was rather than teleporting from step one.
      Framing _previousFraming(Framing current) {
        final index = _framings.indexOf(current);
        if (index <= 0) return current;
        return _framings[index - 1];
      }
      
      double _lerpAngle(double a, double b, double t) => a + (b - a) * t;
      
      class _Tutorial3DViewState extends State<Tutorial3DView>
          with TickerProviderStateMixin {
        late final AnimationController _animController;
        late final AnimationController _cameraController;
        late final AnimationController _inertiaController;
        double _rotX = 0;
        double _rotY = 0;
        double _pitchVelocity = 0;
        double _yawVelocity = 0;
        bool _dragged = false;
      
        Duration get _motionDuration =>
            Duration(milliseconds: 2600 + (widget.tool.id.length % 4) * 180);
      
        void _playFromBeginning() {
          _animController
            ..duration = _motionDuration
            ..reset()
            ..repeat();
        }
      
        void _replayTechnique() {
          _inertiaController.stop();
          setState(() {
            _dragged = false;
            _rotX = 0;
            _rotY = 0;
            _pitchVelocity = 0;
            _yawVelocity = 0;
          });
          _cameraController.forward(from: 0);
          _playFromBeginning();
        }
      
        String _motionLabel() {
          final phase = _animController.value;
          if (phase < .14) return 'Ready position';
          if (phase < .46) return 'Demonstrating stroke';
          if (phase < .65) return 'Returning safely';
          if (phase < .86) return 'Repeating technique';
          return 'Resetting position';
        }
      
        @override
        void initState() {
          super.initState();
          _animController = AnimationController(
            vsync: this,
            duration: _motionDuration,
          );
          _cameraController = AnimationController(
            vsync: this,
            duration: const Duration(milliseconds: 900),
            value: 1,
          );
          _inertiaController = AnimationController(
            vsync: this,
            duration: const Duration(milliseconds: 420),
          );
          _inertiaController.addListener(() {
            // Orbit inertia: a short glide after release feels physical.
            setState(() {
              _rotY += _yawVelocity * _inertiaController.value;
              _rotX = (_rotX + _pitchVelocity * _inertiaController.value)
                  .clamp(-0.9, 0.9);
            });
          });
          if (widget.isPlaying) {
            _playFromBeginning();
          }
        }
      
        @override
        void didUpdateWidget(Tutorial3DView oldWidget) {
          super.didUpdateWidget(oldWidget);
          if (widget.isPlaying != oldWidget.isPlaying) {
            if (widget.isPlaying) {
              _playFromBeginning();
            } else {
              // Park on a readable frame instead of freezing mid-stroke.

            }

          }

                        _dragged = true;

                        _pitchVelocity = -details.delta.dy * 0.008;

  content: |

          );

        }
      
        /// Each loop reads as a compact demonstration: settle at the ready pose,
        /// make the forward stroke, return, repeat on the opposite side, then reset.
        /// A linear sine loop made the original gestures feel like idle wobbling.
        double _stagedTechniqueProgress(double value) {
          final t = value.clamp(0.0, 1.0);
          double between(double start, double end, double from, double to) {
            final local = ((t - start) / (end - start)).clamp(0.0, 1.0);
            return from + (to - from) * Curves.easeInOutCubic.transform(local);
          }
      
          if (t < .14) return 0;
          if (t < .46) return between(.14, .46, 0, .25);
          if (t < .65) return between(.46, .65, .25, .5);
          if (t < .86) return between(.65, .86, .5, .75);
          return between(.86, 1, .75, 1);
        }
      
        /// A dark studio backdrop gives the mesh depth cues even on small phones.
        /// It is deliberately drawn here, outside of ToolGeometry, so the reusable
        /// geometry can still render on cards without the animated lesson chrome.
        void _paintCinematicStage(Canvas canvas, Size size) {
          final center = Offset(size.width * .46, size.height * .30);
          final spotlight = Rect.fromCircle(
              center: center, radius: math.max(size.width, size.height) * .72);
          canvas.drawRect(
            Offset.zero & size,
            Paint()
              ..shader = const RadialGradient(
                center: Alignment(-.16, -.35),
                radius: 1.05,
                colors: [Color(0xFF28495B), Color(0xFF132531), Color(0xFF08131D)],
                stops: [0, .47, 1],
              ).createShader(spotlight),
          );
      
          // A perspective grid is a fixed visual reference: as the object rotates,
          // the depth change is much easier to see than over a flat background.
          final horizon = size.height * .67;
          final floor = Path()
            ..moveTo(0, horizon)
            ..lineTo(size.width, horizon)
            ..lineTo(size.width, size.height)
            ..lineTo(0, size.height)
            ..close();
          canvas.drawPath(
              floor, Paint()..color = const Color(0xFF071018).withValues(alpha: .54));
          final grid = Paint()
            ..color = const Color(0xFF74C9E7).withValues(alpha: .12)
            ..strokeWidth = 1;
          for (var x = -2; x <= 12; x++) {
            final baseX = size.width * x / 10;
            canvas.drawLine(
                Offset(size.width / 2, horizon), Offset(baseX, size.height), grid);
          }
          for (var row = 1; row <= 5; row++) {
            final t = row / 6;
            final y = horizon + (size.height - horizon) * t * t;
            canvas.drawLine(Offset(0, y), Offset(size.width, y), grid);
          }
          canvas.drawLine(Offset(0, horizon), Offset(size.width, horizon),
              Paint()..color = const Color(0xFF9CE4F7).withValues(alpha: .18));
        }
      
        /// A short moving light path makes the intended stroke readable before the
        /// viewer has learned the tool. It stays behind the controls and does not
        /// replace the actual animated mesh.
        void _paintTechniqueTrail(Canvas canvas, Size size, double progress) {
          final phase = progress * 2 * math.pi;
          final center = Offset(size.width * .5, size.height * .54);
          final radius = math.min(size.width, size.height) * .30;
          final angle = -math.pi * .78 + math.sin(phase) * .72;
          final point = Offset(center.dx + math.cos(angle) * radius,
              center.dy + math.sin(angle) * radius * .46);
          final glow = Paint()
            ..color = const Color(0xFFFFC66D).withValues(alpha: .22)
            ..maskFilter = const MaskFilter.blur(BlurStyle.normal, 11);
          canvas.drawCircle(point, 11, glow);
          canvas.drawCircle(point, 3.2, Paint()..color = const Color(0xFFFFD58D));
        }
      
        @override
        bool shouldRepaint(covariant EnhancedTool3DPainter oldDelegate) {
          return oldDelegate.toolId != toolId ||
              oldDelegate.stepIndex != stepIndex ||
              oldDelegate.rotX != rotX ||
              oldDelegate.rotY != rotY ||
              oldDelegate.progress != progress ||
              oldDelegate.framing != framing;
        }
